# Requirements Document

## Introduction

This document specifies the Kanban Task Management application (Version V12). It is a role-based web app with a Flask REST API backend and a mobile-first Next.js frontend. Teams run day-to-day work on boards, columns, and cards, and supervisors and managers oversee progress through analytics without opening every board.

The requirements translate decisions already recorded in `specs/project-brief.md` and the steering documents in `.kiro/steering/`. V10 revises V9 and changes product behaviour in six areas, each recorded as a decision (K to P): Guests read a Board only through a Share_Link (Decision K, Requirement 18); every signed-in page carries an App_Header with a User_Menu that holds Profile and Log out (Decision L, Requirement 19); a User has a separate Username and Email in place of `login_identifier` (Decision M); login takes one `identifier` field (Decision N); a Rate_Limiter protects login and the availability check (Decision O, Requirement 20; registration limiting is deferred by Decision Q); and every page of the signed-in application is a Protected_Route (Decision P). V10 also updates the V9 Playwright criteria to the new login form, the header sign-out, the defined route guard, and the rate-limit case. V9 had added only end-to-end test specifications for sign-in and sign-out (Requirement 1, criteria 19 to 38; Requirement 15, criteria 10 to 15). V8 revised V7 and added no scope beyond the decisions in the Decisions Log. Twenty-eight decisions that resolved gaps in the brief are recorded there: Decisions 1 to 5 (carried from V1), Decisions A to D (carried from V2), Decision E (carried from V4: the Backend uses flask-smorest with marshmallow schemas and a generated OpenAPI document; its numeric `status` rule is superseded by Decision G), Decision F (carried from V5: models declare SQLAlchemy relationships only for real cross-table reads, repositories load related rows explicitly, and serialization belongs to marshmallow schemas), Decision G (carried from V6: the Envelope `status` is a boolean, the message text appears once at the top level, and validation errors carry `error.details`), Decision H (carried from V7: the Frontend is styled with Tailwind CSS and shadcn/ui, themed from the design tokens, and the shared `Base*` components wrap shadcn/ui primitives), Decision J (new in V8: the Inbox and the activity feeds are paged with a `limit` and a `before` cursor; every other list stays unpaged), Decision I (new in V8: the board and the Card modal follow a Trello-style interaction model, Cards move by drag-and-drop only, the font is Inter, and the application is named RevoTask), Decisions K to P (new in V10, listed above), and Decisions Q to T (round 2 within V10: registration rate limiting deferred, avatar upload, the Email shown to staff in the member search and the assignee popover, and the invalid-Token redirect; they amend Decisions L, M, O, and P), and Decisions U to W (round 3 within V10: the avatar routes under `/api/users` and avatars in Comments, Board member lists, candidates, and Card assignees; a local-development reset migration in place of the legacy-account backfill; and a change-password rate limit with a minimum candidate-search length; they amend Decisions M, O, R, and S). Criteria affected by a decision carry an inline label such as "(Decision A)". V11 adds Decision X and Requirement 21 (branding): the RevoTask logo and favicon supplied by the product owner replace the placeholder mark; V11 changes no API, data model, or permission. V12 adds Decisions Y to AF and Requirements 22 to 29: every Board is private and visible only to its owner, its BoardMembers, Managers, and Admins (Decision Y, Requirement 22, reversing Decision A and the Board `visibility` column); the V10 Share_Link becomes the Public_Link, managed only by a Manager or an Admin and read-only for everyone (Decision Z, Requirement 23); a new Invite_Link and BoardJoinRequest flow lets Members and Supervisors ask to join, and only a Manager or an Admin approves (Decision AA, Requirement 24); the Card modal saves every change by itself and loses the Save changes button (Decision AB, Requirement 25, superseding the draft model of Decision I); an author can delete their own Comments and an Attachment removal asks in a modal (Decision AC, Requirement 26); a Member ticks Subtasks on assigned Cards and Card progress is derived from Subtasks (Decision AD, Requirement 27); Inbox rows navigate to their Card or Board and three Notification types are added (Decision AE, Requirement 28); and the Profile page uses a two-column layout on wide screens (Decision AF, Requirement 29). V12 also adds V12 end-to-end criteria to the E2E_Auth_Suite (Requirement 1, criteria 65 to 72) and tests (Requirement 15, criteria 29 to 35). V11 branding (Decision X, Requirement 21) is unchanged.

Out of scope: Google OAuth login (post-MVP, added only after username-or-email and password auth is verified end-to-end), server-side token revocation (deferred past MVP; rate limiting was deferred in V2 and is in scope from V10 for login, the availability check, and change-password, Requirement 20 and Decision W; registration stays unlimited, Decision Q), custom per-board workflow templates beyond the five default columns, and subtasks with their own assignee or date. Stretch features (card `progress` meter, `views_count`, decorative Overview/Notes/Questions tabs) are covered in Requirement 16 and are built only after all other requirements work end-to-end.

This feature requires these data-model additions beyond the original `data-model.md` steering: a nullable `Board.owner_id` (Decision 1), a `BoardMember` entity (Decision A), a nullable unique `Board.share_token` (Decision K), a unique case-insensitive `username` and `email` on User in place of the single `login_identifier` column that Decision C introduced and Decision M removes, a `rate_limit_attempts` table (Decision O), and an `avatar_path` column on User in place of the free-text `avatar_url` column (Decision R). V12 removes `Board.visibility` and adds a nullable unique `Board.invite_token`, a `BoardJoinRequest` entity, and on Notification a nullable `board_id`, `actor_id`, and `join_request_id`, a nullable `card_id`, three new types, and partial unique indexes (Decisions Y, AA, and AE); `Card.progress` becomes a computed field and is never stored (Decision AD).

V14 (draft, not implemented) adds production deployment: Decisions AJ to AN and Requirements 33 to 37. Decision AN is approved; the server type (Open Question 31) is open.

V15 renamed the product RevoBoard (Decisions AO and AP). V16 (draft, not implemented, awaiting owner review) rebuilds the look from `design/kanban-prototype-v2.html`, measured from its rendered pages (`design/reference/v2/`), replaces the Sidebar with an icon rail and a top navigation, adds a landing page on its own host, and adds weekly throughput analytics with a Dashboard: Decisions AQ to AX and Requirements 38 to 44. The detail is in `specs/v16/`.
V17 (draft, not implemented, awaiting owner review) fixes a list of UI defects, moves the product to one origin (landing at `/`, app at `/app`, API at `/app/api`), replaces the V16 icon rail and top navigation with a Sidebar and a board-only Top_Menu, and adds board slugs and a 404 page, Column CRUD, List, Calendar, and Archived views with due badges, a richer Dashboard and My Tasks, user active and availability flags, a new Profile, one Share modal, a rebuilt Card modal, notification preferences with a 15 minute due job, and email activation. Decisions AY to BJ, Requirements 45 to 59, and Open Questions 46 to 67 are new; the detail is in `specs/v17/`.

## Glossary

- **System**: The Kanban Task Management application, consisting of the Backend and the Frontend.
- **Backend**: The Flask REST API, organized as one blueprint per domain, each layered `routes.py → service.py → repository.py → schema.py`.
- **Frontend**: The mobile-first Next.js (App Router, TypeScript) application.
- **Design_Tokens**: The visual reference's CSS custom properties (`--brand`, `--surface-*`, `--ink*`, `--space-*`, `--radius-*`, `--shadow-*`, `--ring`, the `--label-*` pairs) defined once in `frontend/styles/tokens.css`. They are the only source of colors, spacing, radii, and shadows.
- **shadcn/ui**: Accessible UI primitives (built on Radix UI) copied into `frontend/components/ui/` and styled with Tailwind CSS utilities. Only the shared `Base*` components import them.
- **Guest**: A visitor without an account or Token. Can open a Board only through a Public_Link (Decisions K and Z) and sees its columns and cards read-only, never its comments or activity. A Guest cannot list or browse Boards, and can open an Invite_Link page only after signing in (Decision AA).
- **Member**: A signed-in user with the member role.
- **Supervisor**: A signed-in user with the supervisor role. Includes everything a Member can do.
- **Manager**: A signed-in user with the manager role. Includes everything a Supervisor can do.
- **Admin**: A signed-in user with the admin role, a separate administrative track and not a superset of Manager (see Requirement 2 and Decision B).
- **Card_Creator**: A Supervisor, Manager, or Admin. Card creation additionally requires Board_Access to the target Board.
- **login_identifier**: The single unique string column on User that Decision C introduced. Removed in V10 (Decision M); it survives only as a historical term in the V2 to V9 snapshots and in the downgrade of the migration.
- **Username**: The User's unique, case-insensitive handle, 3 to 30 characters from `a-z`, `0-9`, `_`, and `.`, stored in lowercase (Decision M).
- **Email**: The User's unique, case-insensitive email address, valid format, at most 255 characters, stored in lowercase. Required on every account: the V10 migration resets local development data, so no account lacks one (Decisions M and V).
- **Identifier**: The single login form value, either a Username or an Email, sent as the request field `identifier` (Decision N). It is treated as an Email only when it contains `@`.
- **Board**: A project container holding Columns, Cards, and Categories. Every Board is private (Decision Y): it has no `visibility`. It has a nullable `owner_id`, a nullable `share_token` (the Public_Link), and a nullable `invite_token` (the Invite_Link).
- **Share_Token**: The random, unguessable, unique string stored in `Board.share_token` (at least 256 bits from a cryptographically secure source). A null value means the Public_Link is off. The column and the name are unchanged in V12 (Decisions K and Z).
- **Public_Link**: The URL `/shared/{token}` built from a Board's Share_Token (the V10 Share_Link, renamed in V12; "Share_Link" in older text means the same thing). Opens the Shared_View for anyone, signed in or not, and is created, regenerated, and disabled only by a Manager or an Admin (Decision Z).
- **Shared_View**: The read-only Frontend view at `/shared/{token}` of a Board's Columns, Cards, Card details, Categories, and Attachment links, without Comments, Activity, Inbox, or any write control, shown to every visitor of a Public_Link who does not already have Board_Access (Decisions K and Z).
- **Board_Owner**: The Supervisor referenced by a Board's `owner_id`. Sees the Board, edits it as a Supervisor with Board_Staff_Access, manages the Invite_Link, and sees pending Join_Requests but does not decide them (Decisions Y and AA).
- **Board_Member**: A join record `(board_id, user_id)` (stored as `BoardMember`), unique per pair, that attaches a Member or a Supervisor to a Board beyond the Board's `owner_id`. A Manager or an Admin is never a Board_Member (Decision Y). Created by a Manager or an Admin directly, or by an approved Join_Request.
- **Board_Access**: A signed-in user has Board_Access to a non-soft-deleted Board exactly when the user is its Board_Owner, a Board_Member, a Manager, or an Admin (Decision Y). Every other signed-in user gets HTTP 403 and a Guest gets HTTP 401. A Public_Link grants only the Shared_View of Requirement 23, and an Invite_Link grants nothing but the right to ask. Card, Comment, Attachment, Activity_Log, and Category access resolves through the owning Board's Board_Access.
- **Board_Staff_Access**: Write access to a Board's content. A user has Board_Staff_Access when the user is a Manager, an Admin, or a Supervisor who is the Board's Board_Owner or a Board_Member. A Member never has it; a Member's writes are limited to the Assigned_Member rules (Decision 2).
- **Invite_Link**: The URL `/invite/{token}` built from a Board's `invite_token`. It lets a signed-in Member or Supervisor send a Join_Request and grants no access by itself. Generated, regenerated, and disabled by the Board_Owner, a Manager, or an Admin (Decision AA).
- **Join_Request**: A `BoardJoinRequest` row `(id, board_id, user_id, status, decided_by, created_at, updated_at)` with status `pending`, `approved`, or `rejected`. At most one pending row exists per `(board_id, user_id)`. Only a Manager or an Admin decides it (Decision AA).
- **Auto_Save**: The Card modal behaviour of Decision AB: each field, Subtask, and Attachment change is sent by its own request when the user commits it, with a visible Saving, Saved, or error state per field, and no Save changes button.
- **Derived_Progress**: The Card's `progress` integer from 0 to 10 computed from its Subtasks (`done` of `total`), null when there are none, never stored or edited (Decision AD).
- **Column**: An ordered list within a Board that contains Cards.
- **Default_Columns**: The five Columns `Todo`, `In Progress`, `Review`, `Revision`, `Done`, in that order, seeded on every new Board.
- **Card**: A unit of work with a title, description, priority, optional start and end dates, optional Category, optional assignee, Subtasks, and Attachments.
- **Assigned_Member**: The user referenced by a Card's `assignee_id`.
- **Priority**: A Card attribute that is exactly one of `high`, `medium`, or `low`.
- **Subtask**: A checklist item of a Card with a title, `is_done` flag, and Float `order`. Has no assignee and no dates.
- **Attachment**: A link (`url`) or an uploaded file (`file_path` and `mime_type`) belonging to a Card. Never has both empty.
- **Signed_File_URL**: A time-limited, signature-bearing URL for an uploaded Attachment file or an Avatar_Image, valid for 15 minutes.
- **Category**: A per-Board label with a name and nullable color, shown as a filter chip.
- **Comment**: A message on a Card. A top-level Comment has a null `parent_id`. A Reply has a non-null `parent_id`.
- **Reply**: A Comment whose `parent_id` references a top-level Comment.
- **Priority_Comment**: A Comment with `is_priority` set to true.
- **Reply_Count**: The denormalized `reply_count` integer on a top-level Comment.
- **Activity_Log**: The append-only `ActivityLog` table recording state-changing actions.
- **Notification**: A server-created record targeting one user, of type `assigned`, `deadline`, `board_join_requested`, `board_added`, or `board_join_rejected` (Decision AE).
- **Inbox**: The side panel listing the signed-in user's Notifications. Each row is a link to the Card or Board it is about (Decision AE).
- **Side_Panel**: The right-side sliding panel shared by Inbox and Activity. (V17: four views, Inbox, Activity, Members, and Categories, NAV-07.)
- **Sidebar** (removed in V16, Decision AT): The left navigation holding brand, nav (including Inbox, Activity, and Categories where permitted), the Boards list, and team members. It holds no profile entry and no Log out item (Decision L). (V17: redefined as the left panel of global items, NAV-01; see the V17 glossary.)
- **App_Header**: The persistent top bar of every Protected_Route page: the Sidebar toggle below 960 pixels, the page context (on a Board, the name, the "Public link" button for a Manager or an Admin, and the "Invite" button for the Board_Owner, a Manager, or an Admin; there is no visibility icon), the Inbox bell, and the User_Menu trigger (Decision L).
- **User_Menu**: The menu opened from the user icon in the App_Header, holding Profile above Log out (Decision L).
- **Profile_Page**: The Protected_Route `/profile` where the signed-in user edits full name and avatar, sees Username and Email, and changes the password (Decision L).
- **Avatar_Image**: The PNG, JPEG, or WebP picture a user uploads on the Profile_Page, at most 2 MB, stored under a generated file name (Decision R).
- **Avatar_URL**: The value of `avatar_url` in a `user` payload, and of `author_avatar_url`, `avatar_url`, and `assignee_avatar_url` in the Comment, Board member, candidate, and Card payloads of Decision U: a Signed_File_URL for the Avatar_Image, or null when the user has none (Decisions R and U).
- **Initials_Avatar**: The fallback avatar that shows the initials of the user's full name when there is no Avatar_URL or the image fails to load (Decision R). The initials are the first letter of each of the first two words of `full_name`, upper-cased; a one-word name gives one letter and a blank name gives `?` (Decision U).
- **Analytics_Summary**: A pre-aggregated payload of counts, deltas, and overdue totals for a Board.
- **Overdue_Card**: A non-soft-deleted Card whose `end_date` is before today and whose Column is not `Done`.
- **Envelope**: The response shape `{ "status": true, "message": string, "data": payload }` for success and `{ "status": false, "message": string, "error": { "code": string, "details"?: object } }` for failure, where `status` is a boolean that is true exactly when the HTTP status is 2xx, `message` is a non-empty string stated once at the top level, and `error` has no `message` field (Decision G).
- **flask-smorest**: The Flask extension through which every blueprint declares request, query, and response marshmallow schemas and from which the OpenAPI 3 document is generated.
- **Soft_Delete**: Setting `deleted_at` on a row instead of removing the row.
- **Token**: The JWT returned by register and login, identifying the acting user and role, with a fixed 24-hour expiry.
- **Rate_Limiter**: The Backend component that counts attempts in the `rate_limit_attempts` Postgres table and blocks further attempts with HTTP 429 `RATE_LIMITED` (Decision O).
- **Rate_Limit_Window**: The sliding period, 900 seconds by default, over which the Rate_Limiter counts attempts (Decision O).
- **Client_IP**: The client address the Rate_Limiter keys on: the connection's remote address, or the address from `X-Forwarded-For` only through the number of trusted proxies configured in `TRUSTED_PROXY_COUNT` (Decision O).
- **APP_TIMEZONE**: The environment variable holding the application's configured server timezone.
- **E2E_Auth_Suite**: The Playwright specs `frontend/tests/e2e/auth/signin.spec.ts` and `frontend/tests/e2e/auth/signout.spec.ts`, which test sign-in and sign-out against the deployed URL (V9).
- **Test_Account**: A seeded, existing user whose Username or Email and password are supplied to the E2E_Auth_Suite through the environment variables `E2E_USER_IDENTIFIER` and `E2E_USER_PASSWORD` (the variable names are unchanged from V9). The suite assumes a Member account and never creates, edits, or deletes accounts (V9). The suite makes at most one failed login attempt against this account per run (AUTH-E2E-SI-06), far below the lockout threshold, and each successful sign-in resets the pair counter. (V12: the Test_Account `member` is a Board_Member of a seeded Board that has a Card assigned to it, so the V12 criteria 69 and 70 have something to open; the suite still never creates or changes data.)
- **Throwaway_Identifier**: A Username-shaped value generated per test run (`e2e-rl-` plus random lowercase characters) that belongs to no account. The rate-limit test (AUTH-E2E-SI-11) fails logins against it, so the Test_Account is never locked and no account is created (V10; see Decision O).
- **Auth_Page_Object**: The Page Object class `AuthPage` in `frontend/tests/e2e/auth/pages/AuthPage.ts` that holds every locator and action of the login page and the Sign_Out_Control, so the specs contain no raw selectors (V9).
- **User_Menu_Trigger**: The App_Header button with the accessible name "User menu". It is visible exactly while a user is signed in, so the E2E_Auth_Suite uses it as the signed-in indicator (V10).
- **Sign_Out_Control**: The "Log out" menu item of the User_Menu. It is the only sign-out control in the Frontend (Decision L, resolving Open Question 6).
- **Session_Cookie**: The browser cookie `kanban_token` that holds the Token (`lib/session.ts`). A signed-out browser has no such cookie.
- **Generic_Login_Error**: The form-level message "Invalid credentials" that the Backend returns for both an unknown Identifier and a wrong password (`auth_service.login`).
- **Protected_Route**: A Frontend page that requires a Session_Cookie and redirects a visitor without one to `/login`. Every page under the `(app)` route group is one: `/boards`, `/boards/{id}` and its sub-pages, `/profile`, `/admin/users`, and `/invite/{token}` (Decision P, resolving Open Question 5; `/invite/{token}` added in V12, Decision AA). `/login`, `/register`, and `/shared/{token}` are not.
- **Safe_Return_Path**: The value of the `next` query parameter on `/login` when it is a path and query that starts with a single `/`, does not start with `//` or `/\`, has no control character, has at most 512 characters, and starts with `/boards`, `/profile`, `/admin`, or `/invite/` (V12, Decision AA); any other value is ignored (Decision T).
- **Brand_Assets**: The three files the product owner supplied in V11: `favicon.ico`, `logo-revo.svg`, and `logo-text-revo.svg`. After the move: `frontend/app/favicon.ico`, `frontend/public/brand/logo-revo.svg`, and `frontend/public/brand/logo-text-revo.svg`.
- **Logo_Mark**: `logo-revo.svg`, the square mark, `viewBox` 480 by 480.
- **Logo_Full**: `logo-text-revo.svg`, the mark with the "RevoTask" wordmark, `viewBox` 540 by 165.
- **BrandLogo**: The only Frontend component that renders a Logo_Mark or Logo_Full (`components/shared/BrandLogo.tsx`), with the variants `mark` and `full`. Feature code uses it and never writes its own `<img>` or `next/image` for the logo.
- **Embedded_Raster**: The base64 PNG that each Brand_Assets SVG file wraps inside an `<svg>` element. Measured in V11: 640 by 640 pixels in `logo-revo.svg` and 720 by 220 pixels in `logo-text-revo.svg`, 8-bit RGB with no alpha channel and no transparency chunk, so each logo has an opaque background. The `viewBox` (480 and 540 wide) is the size at which the raster is shown at about 1.33 device pixels per CSS pixel.
- **Favicon_File**: `frontend/app/favicon.ico`, a Windows icon file with three images (16, 32, and 48 pixels square, 32 bits per pixel), served by the Next.js file convention.
- **Design_System**: `frontend/styles/tokens.css`, the Tailwind theme alias in `frontend/styles/globals.css`, and the Component_Recipes of Requirement 38. It serves the app, the auth pages, and the landing page alike.
- **Component_Recipes**: The table in Requirement 38 criterion 18 that gives each shared component its height, radius role, padding, type token, and shadow.
- **Icon_Rail**: The white pill-shaped bar that floats at the left of every Protected_Route page at 960 pixels and wider, holding the Home circle and icon buttons with tooltips and no logo. (V17: removed, NAV-08.)
- **App_Frame**: The large white rounded panel on the page canvas that holds the App_Header and the page content.
- **Canvas**: The `--surface-100` page background around the App_Frame and the Icon_Rail.
- **Top_Nav**: The pill-shaped navigation group in the App_Header at 960 pixels and wider. (V17: removed, NAV-08.)
- **Board_Switcher**: The dropdown in the Board_Toolbar that lists the Boards the viewer may see and replaces the Sidebar Boards list.
- **Board_Toolbar**: The row under the App_Header on a Board page: the Board_Switcher, the progress meter, "Public link", and "Invite". (V17: the Public link and Invite buttons are replaced by the Share item of the Top_Menu, SHR-01.)
- **Team_View**: The third view of the Side_Panel (after Inbox and Activity) listing the active Board's members. (V17: becomes the Members view of the Side_Panel, NAV-07.)
- **Site_Host**: The host that serves the landing page (planned `revoboard.belajardeploy.web.id`). (V17: removed with Requirement 42; see Path_Topology.)
- **App_Host**: The host that serves the app and `/api` (planned `app.revoboard.belajardeploy.web.id`). Requests with any other host, such as the server IP and port 8082 or `localhost`, are treated as the App_Host. (V17: removed with Requirement 42; see Path_Topology.)
- **Landing_Page**: The public page served at `/` on the Site_Host.
- **Completed_At**: The nullable `completed_at` timestamp on a Card (Decision AW).
- **Throughput_Series**: A list of weekly counts of Cards that were completed in that week.
- **Path_Topology**: The V17 layout of one origin: the Landing_Page at `/`, every app page under `/app`, and the API at `/app/api`.
- **App_Base**: The path prefix `/app`. Every Protected_Route and every sign-in page lives under it.
- **API_Base**: The path prefix `/app/api` the browser uses for every Backend call in production; nginx removes `/app` before Flask sees the request. Configured in the Frontend as `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL`.
- **Sidebar**: The left navigation panel of V17 with the global items (Dashboard, My Tasks, Boards, Users). It is not the V1 to V15 Sidebar (Boards list, Team members, Log out).
- **Top_Menu**: The menu in the App_Header, shown only while a Board is open, with the items Board, List, Calendar, Activity, Members, Categories, and Share.
- **Board_Slug**: The lowercase ASCII text in `boards.slug` that names a Board in its URL.
- **Not_Found_Page**: The on-brand page of `not-found.tsx` for unknown paths and unknown Board slugs.
- **Done_Column**: The Column named `Done` of a Board. It cannot be renamed or deleted.
- **Archived_Card**: A Card with `archived_at` set. It has a null `column_id` and keeps its `board_id`.
- **Due_State**: The read-only Card field `due_state`: `overdue`, `due_tomorrow`, or null, computed by the Backend in `APP_TIMEZONE`.
- **Open_Card**: A Card that is not soft-deleted, not archived, and not in the Done_Column.
- **Active_User**: A user with `is_active` true. An inactive user cannot sign in.
- **Available_User**: A user with `is_available` true. Only users who are both active and available appear in the member picker and the assignee picker.
- **Verified_User**: A user with `email_verified_at` set.
- **Share_Modal**: The one modal opened from the Share item of the Top_Menu, holding the Invite_Link, the pending Join_Requests, and the Public_Link.
- **ConfirmDialog**: The shared modal `components/shared/ConfirmDialog.tsx`, built on `BaseModal`, that asks before the creates and deletes of FIX-07.
- **Notification_Preference**: A row `(user_id, type, enabled)`; a missing row means enabled.
- **Due_Job**: The CLI command `flask notify-due`, run every 15 minutes.
- **Email_Token**: A single-use, hashed, expiring token for email verification or an email change, stored in `email_tokens`.
- **Mail_Backend**: The transport behind `app/common/mail.py`: `smtp` (Gmail) or `console`.

## Decisions Log

| ID | Decision | Rationale | Where applied |
|----|----------|-----------|---------------|
| 1 | `Board.owner_id` is nullable and references the owning Supervisor. | Supervisor ownership and analytics scope need a real column. | Req 3.2, 3.16 to 3.19, 10.1, 10.2 |
| 2 | **(Amended in V12 by Decision AD: a Member's Subtask rights on an assigned Card are stated as an exact matrix, Requirement 27; the rule itself is not reversed.)** Members move and edit only Cards assigned to them. | Keeps Member authority narrow. | Req 4.8, 4.9, 5.4, 5.5 |
| 3 | The create-card action shows to Supervisor, Manager, and Admin on Manager-authored Comments. | Matches the "create from a manager's comment" workflow. | Req 7.14, 7.15 |

Note on Decision 3: Req 7.14 stands as written (viewer is a Supervisor, Manager, or Admin and the Comment's author is a Manager). The backend, product, and frontend steering documents were aligned to it.
| 4 | Board soft-delete and archive are Manager and Admin only. | Boards are records of real work. | Req 3.6, 3.7, 11.8, 11.9 |
| 5 | Admin permission set: view all, manage users and Boards, create and delete Cards, create Columns; no Comments or priority flags. | Defines the Admin track. | Req 2.4 to 2.6 |
| A | **(Superseded in V12 by Decision Y: every Board is private, there is no public Board and no `visibility`. Kept for history.)** Private Boards require Board_Access (owner, BoardMember, Manager, or Admin). Public Boards stay readable by every signed-in user. | "Private" must gate unrelated signed-in users, not only Guests. Interpretation: the request's wording omitted "private", but the stated problem concerns private Boards, so public Boards remain open to signed-in users. **Confirmed unchanged in V10 (Decision K restates it); the Guest half of this decision, that a Guest may read public Boards, is reversed by Decision K.** | Req 3.4, 3.11 to 3.19, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 11.2, 11.6, 11.11, 11.12, 12 |

Clarification to Decision A (V2): Req 3.4 and 11.2 grant Supervisor writes only as owner or BoardMember, while Req 4 and 6 said "Board_Access", which a Supervisor also holds on any public Board. That wording gap is resolved by the Board_Staff_Access term (Glossary). Supervisor, Manager, and Admin writes (Card create, delete, archive, assign, unassign, edit, move; Subtask and Attachment writes by staff; Category create, rename, delete) require Board_Staff_Access, so an unrelated Supervisor cannot write on a public Board. Members keep Board_Access plus the assignee rule (Decision 2). Reads and Comments keep Board_Access.
| B | Admin is deliberately excluded from commenting and priority flagging (Req 2.5) and from analytics (Req 10.4). Admin is a separate administration track, not a superset of Manager. This is a recorded decision, not an oversight. Future editors must not "fix" it. | Changing it would quietly alter the permission model. | Req 2.5, Req 2 note, 7.3, 10.4 |
| C | One unified `login_identifier` field replaces separate email and username. Uniqueness and login matching are case-insensitive. **Superseded in V10 by Decision M** (separate Username and Email). | One column means one uniqueness check and one login lookup. | Req 1.1 to 1.5, 1.17, Glossary |
| D | Uploaded Attachment files are access-gated through an authenticated endpoint or a Signed_File_URL. No permanent public URL. | A permanent public URL bypasses Board_Access and role checks. | Req 4.18, 4.19, 4.24 to 4.27 |
| E | Every response carries top-level `status` and `message`; the Backend uses flask-smorest with marshmallow schemas, validation failures stay HTTP 400 `VALIDATION_ERROR` (not 422), and the OpenAPI document is generated instead of hand-written. The numeric-`status` and `error.message` parts are superseded by Decision G. | One declaration per endpoint drives validation, serialization, and documentation, so they cannot drift. | Req 13.2 to 13.4, 13.11 to 13.15, 17 |
| F | Models declare SQLAlchemy relationships only for real cross-table reads (`Board.columns`, `Board.members`, `Card.subtasks`, `Card.attachments`, `Comment.replies`), each with `back_populates` (not `backref`), `lazy="raise"`, and no cascade delete. Repositories load related rows explicitly with `selectinload` or `joinedload`. Models have no `to_dict`; marshmallow schemas serialize, because output depends on the viewer. | An accidental lazy load fails loudly instead of becoming a hidden N+1 query, and viewer-dependent serialization stays in one place. | Req 13.16 to 13.18, 14.12, 15.8, 15.9 |
| G | The Envelope is `{ "status": true, "message", "data" }` on success and `{ "status": false, "message", "error": { "code", "details"? } }` on failure. `status` is a boolean, true exactly when the HTTP status is 2xx; the numeric HTTP status travels on the HTTP line only. The message text appears once, at the top level, and `error` has no `message` field. For `VALIDATION_ERROR`, `error.details` maps each field name to a list of message strings from marshmallow. **Amended in V10 by Decision O:** `error.details` also appears on the register `CONFLICT` (per field) and on `RATE_LIMITED` (`retry_after`). Mutating endpoints supply a short descriptive success message (for example "Card moved"); reads default to "OK". Supersedes the status/message part of Decision E. | One shape on both paths gives the frontend one place to unwrap or throw; one message text avoids two strings drifting apart; `error.code` is what code branches on. | Glossary Envelope, Req 1.1, 1.15, 7.2, 10.5, 13.2 to 13.4, 13.11, 13.13 |
| H | The Frontend is styled with Tailwind CSS (v4) and shadcn/ui. Tailwind's theme is mapped to the Design_Tokens, so the default Tailwind palette, spacing scale, and arbitrary values are not used. `BaseButton`, `BaseBadge`, and `BaseModal` stay the only shared primitives and are thin wrappers over shadcn/ui `Button`, `Badge`, and `Dialog`; feature code never imports shadcn/ui directly. The look follows the product owner's visual reference, a light project-management dashboard (white surfaces, navy brand, pastel tags, icon rail with a boards panel, flat columns, bordered cards); the HTML prototype stays the reference for behaviour and content. Spacing, motion, and visual hierarchy may be refined to make it more engaging. | Tailwind and shadcn/ui give consistent utilities and accessible Radix behaviour (focus, keyboard, ARIA), while the token mapping keeps one source of truth for the look and keeps the prototype as the visual reference. | Req 14.5, 14.9, 14.10, 14.13 to 14.15 |
| I | **(Amended in V12 by Decision AB: the draft model, the Save changes footer button, and the "unsaved changes" prompt are superseded; the rest stands.)** The board and the Card modal follow a Trello-style model. The page itself does not scroll; each Column scrolls inside itself with its add-card action pinned at the bottom; the Card modal is large, shows category, Priority, dates, and assignee as chips that open popovers editing a draft, uses one range calendar for the start and end dates, picks the assignee in a searchable popover, and keeps every action in a footer. Cards change Column only by drag-and-drop, using the HTML5 drag-and-drop API as before (a pointer-event rewrite was tried and reverted at the product owner's request). The font is Inter, self-hosted. The navy and white palette stays, with the corner radii kept small. The Sidebar toggle is the logo mark and the application is named RevoTask. | The product owner asked for the Trello board and card experience and a calmer, less rounded look; Atlassian Sans is proprietary, so the nearest open font is used. | Req 3.20 to 3.22, 4.20, 4.21, 4.30 to 4.40, 5.2, 5.13, 7.21, 8.8 to 8.10, 3.23 to 3.25, 4.41 to 4.45, 3.26 to 3.29, 4.46 to 4.50, 6.13, 6.14, 7.22, 10.8, 14.10, 14.16 to 14.26 |
| J | **(Amended in V12 by Decision AB: the sentence that attachment changes in the Card modal wait for Save changes is superseded; the paging rule is unchanged.)** The Inbox, the Board activity feed, and the Card activity list are paged: each takes `limit` (default 20, at most 100) and `before` (the id of the last row already shown) and returns the next older rows in the same response shape; a response with fewer rows than `limit` is the last page. Every other list stays unpaged. | These three lists grow without bound, and the owner asked to load one screen of rows with a Load more action; a cursor on the row id keeps the response shape unchanged, so existing clients keep working. | Req 8.11, 8.12, 9.11, 9.12, 13.19 |
| K | **(Partly superseded in V12 by Decisions Y and Z: the Share_Link is now the Public_Link, managed only by a Manager or an Admin; the "Clarification to Decision A" half, that signed-in users read public Boards, is withdrawn; the Guest rules and the no-comments-no-activity rule stand.)** **Share links replace public browsing for Guests (V10; reverses Req 11.4, 11.5, and 11.7 as written in V2 to V9).** A Guest can no longer list or browse public Boards. A Guest opens a Board only through a Share_Link `/shared/{token}`, read-only: Columns, Cards, Card details, Categories, and Attachment links through Signed_File_URLs scoped to that Share_Token. A Guest never sees Comments or the Activity_Log, even through a Share_Link (the product.md rule stays). `Board.share_token` is nullable, random, unguessable, and unique; null means sharing is off. The Board_Owner, a Manager, and an Admin can enable, disable, and regenerate it; disabling or regenerating invalidates the old link immediately, including outstanding Signed_File_URLs. A private Board may have a Share_Link (an explicit owner action); a private Board still blocks unrelated signed-in users. There is no expiry date in V10. A signed-in user who opens a Share_Link and has Board_Access gets the normal Board view; otherwise the Shared_View. Without a Share_Token, `/boards` and Board pages need sign-in (Frontend redirect to `/login`, Backend 401). **Clarification to Decision A (V10):** every signed-in user, Members included, can still read public Boards; only Guests lose public browsing. | Public browsing exposed every public Board to anyone who could guess the host. A link the owner creates is an explicit, revocable act, and the unguessable token replaces the Board id as the credential. | Req 3.23, 4.26, 6.6, 7.16, 8.5, 11.4, 11.5, 11.7, 11.13, 12.6, 12.7, 13.10, 14.3, 18 |
| L | **Header user menu and Profile (V10; resolves Open Question 6).** A shared App_Header on every Protected_Route carries a User_Menu with Profile above Log out. The Sidebar loses its profile and Log out entries, so there is one entry point. The Profile_Page lets the user edit full name and avatar, shows Username and Email read-only, and changes the password (current and new, at least 8 characters). Log out calls `POST /api/auth/logout` and discards the Token as before. **Amended in V10 round 2 by Decision R:** the avatar is an uploaded image, not an `https` URL. | One place for account actions; the V9 criteria already described sign-out as a header control. | Req 1.29 to 1.36, 3.23, 14.10, 19 |
| M | **(Amended again in V12 round 2 by Decision S: the pending join-request list also carries `email`, to the Board_Owner, a Manager, and an Admin only.)** **Separate Username and Email (V10; supersedes Decision C and resolves Open Question 1).** User has two unique, case-insensitive columns: `username` (3 to 30 characters from `a-z`, `0-9`, `_`, `.`, stored lowercase) and `email` (valid format, at most 255 characters, stored lowercase). `login_identifier` is dropped. Registration shows field-level errors; a duplicate returns 409 with `error.details` per field (`username`, `email`). `GET /api/auth/availability?username=&email=` serves on-blur checks (rate limited; the 409 on submit stays authoritative). Migration: an email-shaped `login_identifier` becomes `email` and a `username` is derived from its local part with a numeric suffix to de-duplicate; a username-shaped one becomes `username` with `email` left null, and that user sees a small blocking Email_Prompt on the next sign-in (a Frontend gate only). **The backfill and the Email_Prompt are withdrawn in V10 round 3 by Decision V.** Board member candidate search (`GET /api/boards/{id}/candidates`) matches `username` by prefix and `email` by exact match only, case-insensitively, and returns `id`, `full_name`, and `username` (**amended in V10 round 2 by Decision S:** the response now also carries `email`, to staff only). | Two real fields give field-level validation and clearer errors; exact-only email matching keeps the search from becoming an address directory by prefix. The user's phrase "username or password" for the search was read as "username or email". | Req 1.1 to 1.5, 1.17, 1.43, 1.47 to 1.50, 1.52 to 1.54, 3.25, 19.13 |
| N | **Login form with one Identifier (V10; breaking).** The login form has one input, "Username or email". The Frontend treats the value as an Email only when it contains `@` and validates the email shape; otherwise it validates the username shape. The Backend request field is renamed from `login_identifier` to `identifier`; the Backend decides the type (email shape: look up by `email`; else it must match the username format: look up by `username`; neither: 400 `VALIDATION_ERROR`). An unknown user and a wrong password return the same generic 401. Register and login return `user` as `{ id, full_name, username, email, avatar_url }`. | One input is simpler to use; deciding the type on the server keeps the rule in one place. | Req 1.1 to 1.3, 1.19 to 1.28, 1.40, 1.44 to 1.46, 1.51 |
| O | **Rate limiting in scope (V10; reverses the V2 deferral in Req 1.18).** Login is blocked after 5 failed attempts per (Identifier, Client_IP) within 15 minutes, plus 20 failed attempts per Client_IP within 15 minutes; a successful login resets that pair's counter. The availability check uses the same Rate_Limiter with its own threshold; registration is not limited (**amended in V10 round 2 by Decision Q**); change-password attempts use the Rate_Limiter too (**amended in V10 round 3 by Decision W**). A blocked request returns HTTP 429, code `RATE_LIMITED`, a `Retry-After` header, and `error.details.retry_after` in seconds. Storage is a Postgres table, so counts hold across workers with no Redis; thresholds and window come from environment variables, and tests can pass small limits without `APP_ENV`. The Frontend shows the wait time and disables submit until it passes. The E2E rate-limit test fails logins against a Throwaway_Identifier, so the shared `E2E_USER_*` account is never locked. **Amends Decision G:** `error.details` also appears on the register `CONFLICT` and on `RATE_LIMITED`; 429 joins the status list. | Credential guessing and address probing were open since V2. A table needs no extra service, and a keyed pair stops one attacker without locking out the real owner of the account from other addresses. | Req 1.18, 13.3, 13.11, 17.1, 20 |
| P | **Route guard (V10; resolves Open Question 5).** Every page of the signed-in application is a Protected_Route. A request without the Session_Cookie `kanban_token` is redirected to `/login` by `frontend/proxy.ts` (the Next.js 16 name for middleware) before the page renders. The check is optimistic (cookie present); the Backend 401 stays the authority. This is Open Question 5 option (b) in effect, and it no longer contradicts Req 11.4 because Decision K removed Guest browsing. **Amended in V10 round 2 by Decision T:** the guard also rejects an unusable cookie, and a server-render 401 ends on `/login` with the cookie removed. | The old open `/boards` existed only to serve Guests. | Req 1.31, 1.33, 14.27, Glossary Protected_Route |
| Q | **Registration rate limiting deferred (V10 round 2; amends Decision O; answers Open Question 8 for register).** `POST /api/auth/register` is not rate limited in V10, and the `register` scope and `RATE_LIMIT_REGISTER_MAX` are removed. Login limiting is unchanged. **Kept, not ordered by the owner, reversible:** the limiter on `GET /api/auth/availability` stays (default 60 per Client_IP per window, `RATE_LIMIT_AVAILABILITY_MAX`) because the endpoint is an enumeration vector (Open Question 14). Registration is protected only by validation and the 409 `CONFLICT`, so `register rate limiting deferred` is a recorded gap: the 409 `error.details` answers the same question as the availability endpoint, and registration also hashes a password per request. | The owner wants to see real abuse before limiting sign-ups, and a limit can lock out a shared office address. Availability is a side-effect-free oracle, so it is the cheaper vector to throttle. | Req 20 (RL-07 withdrawn, RL-10, RL-15, RL-18), Req 15.16, Glossary, Open Question 14 |
| R | **Avatar upload (V10 round 2; amends Decision L; resolves Open Question 10).** The Profile_Page lets the user upload, preview, replace, and remove an Avatar_Image. Accepted: PNG, JPEG, WebP, at most 2 MB (2,097,152 bytes). The Backend decides the type from the leading bytes, never from the extension or the client's Content-Type. The file is stored under a generated name in `UPLOAD_DIR/avatars/`; the original file name is never used or stored. Replacing or removing deletes the old file after the database change commits. Endpoints (moved from `/api/auth/...` in V10 round 3 by Decision U): `POST /api/users/me/avatar` (multipart, field `file`), `DELETE /api/users/me/avatar`, and the signature-checked `GET /api/users/{user_id}/avatar/file`. `avatar_url` in the `user` payload is a Signed_File_URL built by the same signing and file-serving code as Decision D, with the scope `avatar`, and is issued only in responses to signed-in requests. A Guest on a Share_Link receives no `avatar_url` and sees initials (Requirement 18 payloads carry no user data). The V10 column `avatar_url` becomes `avatar_path`; PATCH and the admin user endpoints no longer accept `avatar_url`. | A picture the user owns beats a URL that can break or track; reusing Decision D's signing avoids a second access mechanism. | Req 19.2, 19.8, 19.10, 19.18 to 19.33, 1.6, 13.10, 15.20 to 15.22 |
| S | **(Amended in V12 round 2 within V12 by the owner's answer to Open Question 24: the pending join-request list of the Invite modal is a third staff-only place for the Email; see the end of this row.)** **Email shown to staff in two places (V10 round 2; reverses part of Decision M; resolves Open Question 12).** `GET /api/boards/{id}/candidates` returns `id`, `full_name`, `username`, and `email`, and still matches `full_name`, `username` by prefix, and `email` by exact value, case-insensitively. Only the Board_Owner, a Manager, and an Admin may call it (unchanged). **Amended in V10 round 3 by Decision W:** the search text needs at least 2 characters after trimming (400 `VALIDATION_ERROR` otherwise), which resolves Open Question 16. The assignee popover lists the Board's members with full name and Email through `GET /api/boards/{id}/members?include=email`, which answers 403 to a viewer without Board_Staff_Access; the popover stays hidden from a Member. The Email appears only in those two places in the Frontend and in no other Board-scoped payload: never in Comments, Activity_Log rows, Notifications, the Shared_View, the Sidebar member rows, or any list a Member can read. **Amended in V12 round 2 (Open Question 24):** the Email is also shown in a third staff-only place, the pending join-request list of the Invite modal (`GET /api/boards/{id}/join-requests`), to the Board_Owner, a Manager, and an Admin only, together with the requester's full name and Avatar_Image or Initials_Avatar. The other exclusions stand: the Email never appears in Comments, Activity_Log rows, the Shared_View or any `/api/shared` payload (the Public_Link), Notification text or Notification payloads, the Sidebar member rows, or any list a Member can read. | The owner wants to tell two people with the same name apart when adding or assigning, and an approver needs the Email to recognise a requester. | Req 3.25, 3.30, 3.31, 4.33, 15.24, 24.13, 24.19, 24.27, 15.36 |
| T | **(Amended in V12 by Decision AA: `/invite/{token}` is a Protected_Route and a Safe_Return_Path prefix.)** **Invalid Token redirects to login on every request (V10 round 2; amends Decision P; resolves Open Question 7).** An invalid, malformed, or expired Token on a Protected_Route ends on `/login` with the Session_Cookie removed and a Safe_Return_Path in `next`, for a server render as well as a client call. `frontend/proxy.ts` handles an unusable cookie (not decodable, missing claims, `exp` passed) without a network call and removes the cookie on its redirect. A server render that gets a Backend 401 redirects to the route handler `/session-expired`, which removes the cookie and redirects to `/login`, because a Server Component cannot remove a cookie. Client-side 401 handling is unchanged. The public pages `/login`, `/register`, and `/shared/{token}` ignore a stale cookie, and the shared routes treat an invalid Bearer Token as no Token. | A stale cookie otherwise passes the guard and breaks every page with an error; the owner wants one rule for every request. | Req 1.34, 1.55 to 1.62, 14.28, 15.23, 18.24 |
| U | **Avatar routes under `/api/users` and avatars in viewer-facing payloads (V10 round 3; amends Decision R; resolves Open Question 15).** The avatar routes become `POST /api/users/me/avatar` (multipart, field `file`), `DELETE /api/users/me/avatar`, and the signature-checked `GET /api/users/{user_id}/avatar/file`; `GET|PATCH /api/auth/me` and `POST /api/auth/change-password` stay under `/api/auth`. `avatar_url` (a Signed_File_URL with scope `avatar`, issued only in responses to signed-in requests, Decisions D and R) is added to Comment and Reply authors (`author_avatar_url`), Board member rows and candidates (`avatar_url`), and Card assignees (`assignee_avatar_url`, beside a new `assignee_name`). The Frontend shows the Avatar_Image, or the Initials_Avatar when there is none, in those places: Comments and Replies, the Sidebar Team members, the member search, the assignee popover, the board Card, and the Card modal assignee chip. **Guests:** the Shared_View keeps initials only. A share-scoped avatar URL would need a new signature scope tied to the Share_Token, a new field on the shared Card schema, and a branch in the file route, for a view that is status-only (Decision K), so the simpler option was chosen. Email still appears only where Decision S allows it. No extra query is added: the existing batched author and assignee lookups also select `avatar_path`. | People should be recognisable where they act, not only on their own Profile; the avatar routes belong with the users resource. | Req 19.18 to 19.26, 19.34 to 19.40, 7.21, 7.22, 3.25, 3.30, 3.32, 4.33, 18.11, 18.25, 15.25, 15.26 |
| V | **Local-development reset migration (V10 round 3; amends Decision M; resolves Open Question 11).** The application is not deployed; the only database is a local development one. The V10 migration therefore backfills no `login_identifier` value and has no Email_Prompt. It deletes user data (users and every table that references them), creates `username` and `email` as NOT NULL with the unique lower-case indexes, adds `boards.share_token` and `rate_limit_attempts`, replaces `avatar_url` by `avatar_path`, and drops `login_identifier`. It runs only when `APP_ENV` is `development` or `testing` and aborts otherwise. The developer then re-runs `flask seed-users` or `flask seed-data`, and the seed gives every seeded user a `username`, an `email`, and a `full_name`, including the Test_Account. The sanitized-Username rule, the old-to-new printout, the first-login Email prompt, `email` on `PATCH /api/auth/me`, and the risk that the migration drops `avatar_url` values are removed, because there is no data to preserve. | Writing and testing a data-preserving migration for data that does not exist costs more than resetting a local database. | Req 1.52, 1.53, 1.63, 1.64, 19.11, 19.13, 19.14, 15.18, 15.27, Open Question 11 |
| W | **Change-password limiter, candidate-search minimum, shared-view and risk answers (V10 round 3; amends Decisions O and S; resolves Open Questions 9, 13, 14, 16, 17, and 18).** (a) `POST /api/auth/change-password` is rate limited under the scope `change_password`, keyed by user id, blocking after `RATE_LIMIT_CHANGE_PASSWORD_MAX` (default 5) wrong current passwords in the Rate_Limit_Window (Requirement 20, criteria 19 to 23). (b) `GET /api/boards/{id}/candidates` needs a search of at least 2 characters after trimming; no per-user request limit is added. (c) The Shared_View shows the assignee's `full_name` only, never Username, Email, or avatar; it accepts `search` and `filter[...]` as written. (d) The availability limiter stays at 60 per Client_IP per window and registration stays unlimited (Decision Q); the enumeration that the 409 on `POST /api/auth/register` allows is a recorded, accepted gap. (e) Avatar files keep their EXIF data and the multipart body is capped at the reverse proxy (about 3 MB); both are accepted risks for V10. | The owner agreed to the recommendations recorded for these questions; each one is the smallest change that closes it. | Req 3.25, 3.32, 15.24, 18.11, 18.25, 19.11, 19.16, 20.10, 20.12, 20.19 to 20.23, Open Questions 9, 13, 14, 16, 17, 18 |
| X | **RevoTask logo and favicon (V11; changes Decision I wording only).** The favicon is `app/favicon.ico` (Next.js file convention, no manual `<link>`); the two logos live in `public/brand/` and are shown only through the shared `BrandLogo` component using `next/image` with `unoptimized` (Next.js does not optimize SVG and rejects it by default). Display sizes never exceed the `viewBox` (480 and 540 CSS pixels wide), so the Embedded_Raster is not enlarged. Only `favicon.ico` is supplied: no `icon`, `apple-icon`, or web manifest in V11 (Open Question 20). The Sidebar toggle shows Logo_Full when expanded and Logo_Mark when collapsed. | The owner replaced the placeholder mark; one component keeps size, alt text, and file paths in one place. | Req 21, Req 14.16, 14.19, Req 15.28 |
| Y | **Private-only Boards (V12; reverses Decision A, the Guest-clarification of Decision K, and Requirement 11 criteria 1 to 3, 6, 11 to 13).** Every Board is private. `Board.visibility` and every visibility icon, toggle, filter, and listing rule are removed. A Board is visible to its Board_Owner, its Board_Members (role Member or Supervisor), Managers, and Admins; everyone else gets 403 (401 without a Token). Home lists the Boards where the viewer is owner or Board_Member; a Manager and an Admin see all. Editing rights are unchanged (Board_Staff_Access, the Assigned_Member rule, Admin cannot comment or see analytics). A Manager or an Admin is never a Board_Member. Direct add and the candidate search are for a Manager or an Admin only and list only Member and Supervisor accounts; the owner adds people only through Join_Requests. A Card's assignee must be the owner or a Board_Member. The Sidebar Team members list starts with the owner, tagged "Owner", with no remove action on that row (V12 round 2, Open Question 23). The V12 migration drops the column and resets the local development database with no backfill (as in Decision V). | The owner wants Boards to be invitation-only; a public flag no longer has a use once a revocable Public_Link exists. | Req 22, 3.9 to 3.12, 3.20 to 3.25, 3.29, 4.11, 11, 12.7, 15.6 |
| Z | **Public_Link (V12; amends Decision K).** The V10 Share_Link is renamed Public_Link. The column `Board.share_token`, the routes `/api/boards/{id}/share...` and `/api/shared/{token}...`, and the page `/shared/{token}` keep their names, so no URL or column changes. Only a Manager or an Admin creates, regenerates, disables, or reads it (the Board_Owner no longer can). It is read-only for everyone who opens it, a signed-in user without Board_Access included; a signed-in user with Board_Access is redirected to the normal Board. Comments, Activity, avatars, Emails, and owner and member data stay out. No expiry. Every write endpoint answers 401 or 403 to such a viewer. | The owner wants publication to be a staff decision and the link to work for any viewer. Keeping the column and routes avoids a rename migration for no behavioural gain. | Req 23, 18.2 to 18.7, 18.17, 18.21, 18.22 |
| AA | **Invite_Link and Join_Requests (V12).** A second token, `Board.invite_token` (nullable, unique, 256 bits), is generated, regenerated, and disabled by the Board_Owner, a Manager, or an Admin from an Invite button that opens a modal. `/invite/{token}` needs sign-in (a Guest signs in or registers and returns). A signed-in Member or Supervisor sees the Board name and a Request to join button; a Manager or an Admin is told they already have access; a member is told they are already a member. A new table `BoardJoinRequest` holds the requests, with one pending request per user and Board. Only a Manager or an Admin approves or rejects; the owner sees the pending list, each row with the requester's Email, full name, and avatar (V12 round 2, Decisions M and S), but does not decide. Approval creates the Board_Member and the user keeps their account role. A rejected user may ask again with no cooldown. Only Member and Supervisor accounts can ask. Disabling or regenerating the Invite_Link leaves pending requests pending. | Owners need a way to bring people in without being able to grant access alone, and the Public_Link must stay read-only. | Req 24, 1.65 to 1.72 |
| AB | **Card modal Auto_Save (V12; supersedes the draft model of Decision I and the "wait for Save changes" sentence attributed to Decision J).** The Save changes button and the draft are removed. Title and description save on blur or Enter (Ctrl or Cmd+Enter in the description); each chip popover saves when its value is confirmed or closed; Subtasks, Comments, and Attachments save at once. Each save is one request with one Activity_Log row (replacing "one save, one activity row"). The footer keeps Archive and Delete. Closing while a save is in flight waits for it; a failed field stays editable with an inline error and Retry, and closing with a failed field asks first. Concurrent edits: last write wins, no version check. | A draft model hid saves behind one button and lost work on a stray close; per-field saving matches how the user thinks and gives per-field history. | Req 25, 4.21, 4.30, 4.32, 4.35, 4.43, 4.44, 8.9 |
| AC | **Comment deletion and Attachment removal confirmation (V12).** An author may delete their own Comment or Reply after a confirmation modal; deleting a Reply decrements the parent's `reply_count` in the same transaction, deleting a top-level Comment deletes its Replies, and a `comment.deleted` Activity_Log row (no text) is written. Hard delete of Comment rows is allowed because a Comment has no `deleted_at` (Req 4.29); the append-only log is untouched. A Manager or an Admin deleting someone else's Comment is out of scope. Removing an Attachment asks in a `BaseConfirm` modal and then deletes at once. `BaseConfirm` popover (small, anchored): Delete Card, Delete Subtask, Remove Board member, Regenerate or Disable a link, Remove profile picture. `BaseConfirm` modal (centred dialog): remove an Attachment, delete a Comment. | Authors need to retract what they wrote; a file removal cannot be undone. | Req 26, 4.44, 7 |
| AD | **Subtask rights and derived progress (V12; clarifies Decision 2).** A Member may toggle Subtasks of a Card assigned to that Member; Requirement 4 criteria 12 to 14 already gave the Assigned_Member the whole Subtask edit set, so the matrix in Requirement 27 criterion 1 keeps that and adds no new right. `progress` is computed from Subtasks (`round half up of 10 * done / total`, null with no Subtasks) and is read-only; it is not a stored column and cannot be posted. | The brief assumed Decision 2 blocked Subtask toggles; the spec did not, so only the statement and the tests are new. A typed progress value would disagree with the checklist. | Req 27, 4.12 to 4.14, 16.1, 16.2 |
| AE | **Inbox navigation and board notifications (V12; amends Requirement 9).** Each Inbox row is a link that marks the Notification read and opens `/boards/{id}?card={card_id}` (Card types), `/boards/{id}?invite=1` (join requests for the owner, Manager, and Admin), `/boards/{id}` (added), or `/boards` (rejected). A missing, archived, or inaccessible target shows a short message, never a blank page. New types: `board_join_requested`, `board_added`, `board_join_rejected`. Notification gains `board_id`, `actor_id`, `join_request_id`, and a nullable `card_id`; deduplication uses three partial unique indexes instead of one composite unique constraint, because a unique constraint treats NULLs as distinct. | A notification that cannot be opened is a dead end; null card ids would otherwise defeat the old unique constraint. | Req 28, 9.1, 9.5 |
| AF | **Profile page layout (V12; amends Decision L layout only).** Two equal columns inside a full-width container from 960 pixels (avatar and account left, change password right), one column below, with the existing tokens. | The page used a narrow centred column that wasted wide screens. | Req 29, 19 |
| AG | **Role tag in the assignee picker (V13; amends Requirement 4 criterion 33).** Each row of the assignee popover, and each Team members row, shows a role tag ("Supervisor" or "Member"). The members list returns `role`. | The picker listed names only, and two people with similar names could not be told apart by role. | Requirement 30. |
| AH | **Control sizes in the Invite and Public link controls (V13; applies Decision H and Requirement 14 criterion 20).** Header buttons and the modal buttons use the 40 pixel control size; compact (32 pixel) only in a dense row; the link field and Copy share one height. | The V12 build used compact buttons beside 40 pixel inputs, which broke the one-scale rule. | Requirement 31. |
| AI | **Document titles (V13; amends BRAND-11).** Every title is `RevoTask | <name>` of the open page or panel, built with Next.js `metadata` and `generateMetadata`. | The owner wants the tab to say the brand and where the user is. | Requirement 32. |
| AJ | **Production topology (V14).** One Ubuntu 24 server, Docker Compose for the apps, host PostgreSQL and host Nginx, one origin, `network_mode: host`. | The owner's deployment brief. One origin replaces the two-domain assumption. | Requirement 33 |
| AK | **Build on the server, test first in CI (V14).** GitHub Actions tests and smoke-tests a separate environment; only then does a push to `master` deploy over SSH, with rollback. | The owner has no registry account and wants a tested build before production. | Requirement 34 |
| AL | **Health endpoint (V14).** A public `GET /api/health` reports whether the database answers. | Container, smoke, and deploy checks need one signal. | Requirement 35 |
| AM | **Manual demo seed (V14).** `seed-data` runs once by hand in a one-off container with `APP_ENV=development`; the running app stays `production`. | The owner wants demo data and keeps the seed guard (option A). | Requirement 36 |
| AN | **Migrations on an empty production database (V14, approved in round 2; amends Decisions V and Y).** The V10 and V12 reset migrations may run outside development only when `users` is empty, and then truncate nothing. | Both migrations abort in production today, so upgrade on start cannot create the database. | Requirement 37; amends USR-13 and ACC-17 |
| AO | **Product renamed RevoTask to RevoBoard, new logo (V15; supersedes Decision X's asset content, not its file paths).** New `logo-revo.svg` (mark, viewBox 203 by 203) and `logo-text-revo.svg` (full, viewBox 704 by 121) replace the V11 files at the same paths; both still wrap a raster PNG, now with real alpha transparency, cropped from a reference image the owner supplied via Stitch. `favicon.ico` is regenerated (16/32/48 px) from the same mark. Every visible string "RevoTask" (alt text, Sidebar tooltip, header text, the document-title brand, root metadata title and description) becomes "RevoBoard". | The owner commissioned a "Kanban Task Management App" design in Stitch and chose its brand over the placeholder mark. | Requirement 21 (BRAND-03, 06, 07, 10, 11), Requirement 32 (document titles), Glossary |
| AP | **Teal becomes a real accent colour (V15).** `--accent` changes from `#f4a340` (orange) to `#006b5f` (teal), matching the Stitch design's secondary colour. The token stays the single named accent; no `--accent-2` token is added, and no other token changes. | The owner reviewed the Stitch redesign report and chose a real teal accent over staying navy-only with pastel tags. | Requirement 14 (design tokens); `styles/tokens.css` |
| AQ | **One design system, measured from the prototype (V16; amends Decisions H and I).** `frontend/styles/tokens.css` stays the single source of every colour, type, spacing, radius, shadow, size, layer, duration, and opacity value. It is extended with the prototype's palette, type scale, and radius scale. No second token set exists for the landing page. Every component follows one component recipe table (height, radius, padding, type, fill, shadow) whose values were measured on the prototype's canonical pages (1, 5, 10, 11, 12, 13); pages 3, 4, 6, 7, 8, and 9 are redrawn with the same recipes because they use a different colour set and radii. | The owner wants every component to look balanced next to the others, and a marketing-only layer would let the landing page drift from the app. | Requirement 38 |
| AR | **Typography (V16; amends Decision I, Requirement 14 criterion 17).** Headings use Plus Jakarta Sans and everything else uses Inter, both self-hosted with `@fontsource-variable` and no third-party font request. The prototype's type scale is the scale. | The prototype sets headings in Plus Jakarta Sans. Two self-hosted families keep the no-third-party-request rule. | Requirement 38 |
| AS | **Radius, size, and elevation (V16; supersedes the 8 and 10 pixel radius limit of Requirement 14 criterion 19, the 40 and 32 pixel control sizes of Requirement 14 criterion 20 and V13, and Decision I).** Radius is a role scale measured on the prototype: sm 8, field 12, card 16, frame 24 pixels, and full (pills and circles). Primary actions are teal pills, 36 pixels high; compact pills are 28; chips are 24; fields are 40. Shadows are a named scale of seven. | The prototype uses larger radii and pills. A role scale keeps them consistent instead of per-component. | Requirement 38 |
| AT | **App shell (V16; supersedes the Sidebar of Decisions I and L, Requirement 14 criteria 6, 7, 16, 21, 24, and 26, and the Sidebar parts of Requirements 3, 6, 8, 9, 19, 21).** The Sidebar is replaced by an Icon_Rail, a Top_Nav of pills, a Board_Switcher dropdown, and a Team view in the Side_Panel. Log out and Profile stay in the User_Menu. | The owner chose the prototype shell. | Requirement 39 |
| AU | **Landing page (V16; resolves Open Question 36).** A public, static, server-rendered page on the site host, built from the shared components, with no API call and no session read. | The owner wants a promotion page and wants the app on its own host. | Requirement 41 |
| AV | **Two hosts (V16; amends DEP-05 and DEP-13).** The site host serves the landing page; the app host serves the app and `/api`. One Next.js process serves both and chooses by the `Host` header. The Session_Cookie stays host-only on the app host. | The owner wants the front end moved to `app.<domain>`. One process keeps the deploy as it is. | Requirement 42 |
| AW | **Throughput and the Dashboard (V16; amends Requirement 10).** A Card records `completed_at`, set when it enters a Column named `Done` and cleared when it leaves. Two read-only endpoints return completed Cards per week. A Dashboard page shows them for Supervisors and Managers. | The owner wants the prototype's throughput chart. The current `done_delta` guesses from `updated_at`, which any edit changes. | Requirement 43 |
| AX | **Prototype exclusions (V16; extends the V15 list).** Features the prototype draws that have no spec basis stay unbuilt (the list below). | Keeps scope to the existing features, as the owner asked. | Requirements 39 to 43 |
| AY | **UI fixes and one confirmation dialog (V17).** Auth pages use the same canvas and surface tokens as Boards, a transparent logo, per-field error clearing, and whole-form validation on submit. Select highlights follow the pointer. The Team panel gets a spacing token. One shared `ConfirmDialog` over `BaseModal` asks before every create and delete listed in FIX-07. It replaces the `BaseConfirm` popover and modal variants (amends Decision AC). | The owner listed seven defects; one confirmation component keeps destructive and creating actions consistent. | Requirement 45 |
| AZ | **Path topology (V17; supersedes Decision AV and Requirement 42).** One Next.js app serves `/` and `/app/...`. nginx maps `/app/api/*` to Flask `/api/*`; Flask routes do not change. Host routing is removed. | The owner wants one origin and one deploy without a second DNS name or certificate. | Requirement 46 |
| BA | **Sidebar, Top_Menu, and bell (V17; supersedes Decision AT and Requirement 39 except the App_Header, Board_Switcher, Board_Toolbar progress pill, Skip link, and Side_Panel look).** A Sidebar holds global items; a Top_Menu holds board items; notifications are reached from the bell only; Categories and Members are Side_Panel views. | Global and board navigation were mixed in one list; the owner wants them apart. | Requirement 47 |
| BB | **Board slug and the 404 page (V17).** A Board has a unique `slug` generated from its name and regenerated on rename; the old slug is not kept. The frontend Board URL is `/app/b/{slug}`; every other endpoint keeps the Board UUID. | Readable URLs. A kept old slug would need a redirect table the owner declined (OQ46). | Requirement 48 |
| BC | **Column CRUD and Column-less archived Cards (V17; amends Requirement 3 criteria 4 and 5 and Requirement 2 criterion 6).** The Board_Owner, a Manager, and an Admin create, rename, reorder, and delete Columns; a Card in `Done` cannot be archived. `Done` is locked. Archiving sets `cards.column_id` to null and a Card carries its own `board_id`. | The owner wants Board_Owner, Manager, and Admin control of Columns and archived Cards that never block a Column delete. | Requirement 49 |
| BD | **Board views and due badges (V17).** List, Calendar, and Archived views under `/app/b/{slug}/...`. The Card payload carries a read-only `due_state` computed in `APP_TIMEZONE`. | Different ways to read the same Cards; one server-side rule for "overdue" and "due tomorrow". | Requirement 50 |
| BE | **Dashboard and My Tasks (V17; supersedes DASH-08 and DASH-09, amends Requirement 43).** The Dashboard gets a greeting, filters, and new widgets from one endpoint. My Tasks lists a user's assigned Cards in time groups. | The owner wants a working overview and a personal task list. | Requirements 51 and 52 |
| BF | **Users: active, available, no delete (V17; supersedes the Admin user delete and amends Requirement 1 criteria on users and Decision B only in that an inactive user is refused).** Two flags, a table screen, and a deactivation rule that protects open work. | Deleting a user breaks history and assignments; deactivation keeps them. | Requirement 53 |
| BG | **Profile (V17; supersedes Requirement 29 and Decision AF).** A social-style page with About, Security, and Notifications tabs; username and email become editable; an email change is confirmed by link. | The owner wants a fuller account page and safe email change. | Requirement 54 |
| BH | **One Share modal and a rebuilt Card modal (V17; supersedes SHELL-17 and the two-modal split of Requirements 23, 24, and 31 for layout only).** Behaviour, permissions, and payloads are unchanged. | One entry point for sharing; the owner supplied new references. | Requirements 55 and 56 |
| BI | **Notification preferences and the due job (V17; supersedes Requirement 9 criteria 2 and 3).** Five preference types; the daily `deadline` job is replaced by `flask notify-due` every 15 minutes. | Assignees choose what reaches them; the day-level job could not catch changes made during the day. | Requirements 57 and 58 |
| BJ | **Account activation and mail (V17; amends Requirement 1 and Requirement 19).** Registration sends a verification link; sign-in needs a verified email; mail goes through Gmail SMTP behind a small transport. | The owner wants verified addresses and a safe email-change flow. | Requirement 59 |
| BK | **Toasts (V18; supersedes inline success messages and non-field error banners for action results).** One `sonner` Toaster at the bottom right; field errors, page states, auto-save status, and an open ConfirmDialog's error area stay. | One predictable place for feedback. | Requirement 60 |
| BL | **Design consistency scales (V18).** Spacing, control-height, chip-height, and progress tokens; one searchable combobox, one date field, one checkbox; no option highlighted on open. | The owner found inconsistent space and sizes across the project. | Requirement 61 |
| BM | **Admin reads the Dashboard (V18; amends Decision B and Requirement 10 criterion 4, DASH-19).** An Admin has the Manager's analytics scope; a Supervisor keeps only owned Boards. An Admin still cannot comment or be assigned. | The owner wants the Admin to see the whole picture. | Requirement 65 |
| BN | **Managers and Admins are never assignees (V18; amends TASK-01 and Requirement 4 assignment).** My Tasks is for Member and Supervisor. | A Manager cannot hold Cards, so My Tasks would always be empty. | Requirement 66 |
| BO | **Column drag (V18; amends COL-11).** Columns reorder by drag on the existing card drag code; the menu stays as the keyboard way. | Same gesture as Cards. | Requirement 67 |
| BP | **Per-item Member rights (V18; supersedes the assigned-Member edit rule of Requirement 4).** An Assigned Member edits only the description, adds subtasks, attachments, and comments, edits and deletes only their own subtasks and attachments, checks any subtask, and still moves the Card. Subtasks and attachments get `created_by_id`. | A Member must not change what others wrote. | Requirement 69 |
| BQ | **Who comments (V18; amends Requirement 7).** A Manager, a Supervisor with Board_Staff_Access, and the Card's assignee Member. Not an Admin (Decision B) and not any other Member. | Comments come from people responsible for the Card. | Requirement 69 |
| BR | **Category palette (V18; amends Requirement 8).** `color` is one of 18 palette keys validated by the Backend; the five V17 keys keep their names. | Five colours were too few; a free picker breaks the token rule and contrast. | Requirement 71 |

Notes on status codes: V2 standardizes "no Token returns 401; valid Token with insufficient role or access returns 403". This changes Req 11.5 (a Guest requesting a private Board) from 403 in V1 to 401 in V2. From V10 a request without a Token returns 401 on every Board route, public Boards included; the only Token-free Board reads are the shared routes of Requirement 18. V10 adds 429 for a blocked rate-limited request (Decision O). V12 (Decision Y): a signed-in user without Board_Access gets 403 on every Board route, because no Board is public; `/api/invite` needs a Token (401) and `/api/shared` needs none.

## Open Questions

1. **login_identifier format validation (RESOLVED in V10, Decision M):** `login_identifier` no longer exists. Username and Email each have a defined format (Requirement 1, criteria 47 and 48). The V2 provisional behaviour is withdrawn.
2. **Attachment delivery cost:** Signed time-limited URLs or an authenticated proxy cost more to implement than a public URL. Flagged for the timeline and estimate owner.
3. **Decision A interpretation (OBSOLETE in V12: Decision Y removes public Boards; see Requirement 22):** Public Boards stay readable by every signed-in user. Decision K restates this and removes only the Guest half.
4. **Swagger UI assets from a CDN:** Under flask-smorest, Swagger UI loads its assets from a CDN by default. This is acceptable in development and testing, where the docs routes exist. Flag if offline documentation is required.
5. **Route guard (RESOLVED in V10, Decision P):** every page under the `(app)` route group is a Protected_Route, guarded by `frontend/proxy.ts`. Criteria 1.31 and 1.33 are no longer pending, and their tests are no longer `test.fixme`.
6. **Sign-out control location (RESOLVED in V10, Decision L):** sign-out is the "Log out" item of the User_Menu in the App_Header; the Sidebar no longer has it.
7. **Invalid Token on a server render (RESOLVED in V10 round 2, Decision T; originally V9):** the redirect and the cookie removal are specified in Requirement 1, criteria 55 to 62. The original question:  `lib/api.ts` redirects to `/login` on a 401 only for client-side calls made while a Session_Cookie is present. Whether a server render (`serverGet`) of a Protected_Route with an invalid or expired Session_Cookie redirects, shows an error, or fails is not specified; `frontend/proxy.ts` checks only that the cookie exists. Recommendation: redirect to `/login` on a 401 from a server render (the stale cookie is overwritten by the next sign-in). Criterion 1.34 covers the client-side call only.
8. **Rate-limit thresholds for register and availability (RESOLVED for register in V10 round 2, Decision Q):** registration is not rate limited. The availability threshold of 60 is still a proposal and is Open Question 14.
9. **Change-password brute force (RESOLVED in V10 round 3, Decision W):** `POST /api/auth/change-password` is rate limited under its own scope (Requirement 20, criteria 19 to 23). The original question: the endpoint takes the current password but was not covered by the Rate_Limiter in V10, because Decision O named login, register, and availability only.
10. **Avatar editing method (RESOLVED in V10 round 2, Decision R):** the user uploads an image (PNG, JPEG, or WebP, at most 2 MB) on the Profile_Page; the `https` URL rule is withdrawn (Requirement 19, criteria 18 to 33).
11. **Legacy accounts with identifiers that fit neither shape (RESOLVED in V10 round 3, Decision V):** the application is not deployed and the only data is on a local development machine, so the migration resets that data and re-seeding recreates it; there is no backfill, no sanitized Username, and no Email_Prompt (Requirement 1, criteria 52, 53, 63, and 64).
12. **Candidate search by name (RESOLVED in V10 round 2, Decision S):** name matching stays, and the candidate response and the assignee popover show the Email to staff. Open Question 16 records the exposure this creates.
13. **Shared_View scope details (RESOLVED in V10 round 3, Decision W):** the Shared_View shows the assignee's `full_name` only (Requirement 18, criteria 11 and 25), never Username, Email, or Avatar_URL, and accepts `search` and `filter[...]` on the shared Card list as written. Consistency note: the shared Card payload keeps the opaque `assignee_id` that the Card list already carries, which is why `filter[assignee]` is accepted, but a Guest has no member list, so the shared filter row offers search and Category only.
14. **Availability limiter kept (RESOLVED in V10 round 3, Decision W):** the limiter on `GET /api/auth/availability` stays at 60 per Client_IP per window, and registration stays unlimited (Decision Q). Because `POST /api/auth/register` answers the same question with a 409, an attacker can still enumerate Usernames and Emails through it; this is a recorded, accepted gap for V10, to be revisited together with registration limiting.
15. **Avatar visibility (RESOLVED in V10 round 3, Decision U):** `avatar_url` is also returned for Comment and Reply authors, Board members, candidates, and Card assignees, to signed-in viewers only; Guests on a Share_Link see initials (Requirement 19, criteria 34 to 40).
16. **Email exposure through candidate search (RESOLVED in V10 round 3, Decision W):** the search needs at least 2 characters after trimming (Requirement 3, criterion 25). Email only for Manager and Admin and a per-user request limit were not chosen; a Board_Owner can still read the Email of an account whose name matches a 2-character search, which is the accepted residue of Decision S.
